prose-gray dark:prose-invert [contain:inline-size] isolate empty:hidden mb-14" data-page-title="Hosting the Agent SDK" data-page-href="/en/agent-sdk/hosting" id="content"><span data-as="p">The Agent SDK spawns and supervises a <code>claude</code> CLI subprocess that owns a shell, a working directory, and session files on disk. Hosting it is not like hosting a stateless API wrapper. Every running agent is a long-lived process tied to local state, which shapes how you allocate resources, persist sessions, and scale across tenants.</span>
<span data-as="p">This page covers self-hosting on your own infrastructure. For deployable Dockerfiles and Kubernetes manifests, see the <a href="https://github.com/anthropics/claude-cookbooks/tree/main/claude_agent_sdk/hosting" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">hosting cookbook</a>.</span>
<span data-as="p">If you don’t need to run the agent loop itself on your own infrastructure, consider <a href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/managed-agents/overview" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">Managed Agents</a> instead. Anthropic hosts the agent loop, and your application sends events and receives streamed results through the client SDKs or the REST API. Tool execution runs in an Anthropic-managed cloud sandbox or a <a href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/managed-agents/self-hosted-sandboxes" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">self-hosted sandbox</a> on your own infrastructure.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="the-subprocess-model" aria-labelledby="_R_79deehkd74t9_"><a href="#the-subprocess-model" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: The subprocess model"></a><span id="_R_79deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">The subprocess model</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">Every hosting decision on this page follows from how the SDK runs the agent. When your code calls <code>query()</code>, the SDK spawns a separate <code>claude</code> CLI process and talks to it over stdio. That subprocess owns the shell, the working directory, and the JSONL session transcripts on local disk.</span>

<span data-as="p">One agent session maps to one subprocess. Running N concurrent sessions takes N subprocesses, each with its own process tree and transcript file. By default they all inherit your application’s working directory. When sessions need separate working directories, pass a distinct <code>cwd</code> in the options of each session’s <code>query()</code> call:</span>

<span data-as="p">The TypeScript examples on this page use top-level <code>await</code>, so save them as <code>.mts</code> files or set <code>&quot;type&quot;: &quot;module&quot;</code> in <code>package.json</code>.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="state-that-lives-on-local-disk" aria-labelledby="_R_l9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#state-that-lives-on-local-disk" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: State that lives on local disk"></a><span id="_R_l9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">State that lives on local disk</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Three kinds of agent state live on the container’s filesystem by default. None of them survive a container restart, a scale-down, or a move to a different node.</span>
<!-- --><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px" data-component-part="scroll-area" data-table-wrapper="true" class="min-w-0 [--page-padding:20px] flex w-[calc(100%+(var(--page-padding)*2))] my-[1em] py-[1em] -mx-(--page-padding) max-w-none [contain:inline-size]"><div data-id="base-ui-_R_p9deehkd74t9_-viewport" tabindex="-1" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" style="overflow:scroll" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable table"><div role="presentation" style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="flex"><div class="px-(--page-padding) grow max-w-none table"><table class="m-0 min-w-full w-full max-w-none table [&amp;_th]:text-left [&amp;_td[data-numeric]]:tabular-nums [&amp;_td]:min-w-[150px]"><thead><tr><th>State</th><th>Default location</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Session transcripts</td><td><code>~/.claude/projects/</code>, or the <code>projects/</code> directory under <code>CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR</code> if set</td></tr><tr><td><code>CLAUDE.md</code> memory files</td><td><code>~/.claude/CLAUDE.md</code> for the user tier and the session’s working directory for the project tier</td></tr><tr><td>Working-directory artifacts</td><td>The session’s working directory</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">To persist transcripts across hosts, configure a <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/session-storage" class="link"><code>SessionStore</code> adapter</a>. Memory files and other working-directory artifacts need their own storage strategy, such as a mounted volume or an object-store sync.</span>
<span data-as="p">For how sessions, resumption, and forking work at the API level, see <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/sessions" class="link">Sessions</a>.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="choose-a-session-pattern" aria-labelledby="_R_v9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#choose-a-session-pattern" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Choose a session pattern"></a><span id="_R_v9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Choose a session pattern</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">These four patterns cover session lifecycle: how long a container lives relative to the sessions it serves. For where the container runs, the <a href="https://github.com/anthropics/claude-cookbooks/blob/main/claude_agent_sdk/07_Hosting_the_agent.ipynb" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">hosting cookbook</a> has <a href="https://github.com/anthropics/claude-cookbooks/tree/main/claude_agent_sdk/hosting" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">deployable code</a> for local Docker, Modal, and Kubernetes. Choose a session pattern here and a deployment target from the cookbook.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="ephemeral-sessions" aria-labelledby="_R_139deehkd74t9_"><a href="#ephemeral-sessions" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Ephemeral sessions"></a><span id="_R_139deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Ephemeral sessions</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Create a container for each user task and destroy it when the task completes. Best for one-off tasks. The user may still interact with the AI while the task is completing, but once completed the container is destroyed.</span>
<span data-as="p">Example workloads include bug investigation and fix, invoice and receipt extraction, document translation, and media transformation.</span>
<span data-as="p">The container runs a one-shot entrypoint that reads the task from the <code>TASK_PROMPT</code> environment variable, calls the SDK, and exits.</span>

<span data-as="p">The script prints each message as it arrives, including a result message whose <code>subtype</code> is <code>success</code> when the task completes within the turn limit. If the task hits the 20-turn limit instead, the result message’s <code>subtype</code> is <code>error_max_turns</code> and the <code>query()</code> call raises an error after yielding it, so wrap the loop in a try block if the container needs to exit cleanly. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/agent-loop#handle-the-result" class="link">Handle the result</a> for the error subtypes.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="long-running-sessions" aria-labelledby="_R_1f9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#long-running-sessions" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Long-running sessions"></a><span id="_R_1f9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Long-running sessions</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Run persistent container instances, often hosting multiple SDK processes per container, to serve ongoing work. Best for agents that take autonomous action, serve content, or handle high-volume message streams.</span>
<span data-as="p">Example workloads include an email agent that triages and responds to incoming mail, a site builder that hosts a per-user editable site through container ports, and a chat bot that handles continuous traffic from a platform like Slack.</span>
<span data-as="p">The container exposes an HTTP or WebSocket endpoint and maps each active session to a long-lived query and the subprocess behind it. The calls that keep sessions open and warm differ between the SDKs:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>TypeScript</strong>: use <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#query-object" class="link"><code>streamInput()</code></a> to add turns to an active session. Call <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#startup" class="link"><code>startup()</code></a> to pre-warm subprocesses ahead of incoming traffic. If you don’t know a session’s working directory until its first request arrives, pre-warm with <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#prewarm" class="link"><code>prewarm()</code></a> instead.</li>
<li><strong>Python</strong>: use <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#claudesdkclient" class="link"><code>ClaudeSDKClient</code></a> to hold a session open across turns.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">Size the container so it can hold the maximum number of concurrent sessions in memory.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="hybrid-sessions" aria-labelledby="_R_1r9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#hybrid-sessions" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Hybrid sessions"></a><span id="_R_1r9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Hybrid sessions</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Ephemeral containers that hydrate from a <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/session-storage" class="link"><code>SessionStore</code></a> on startup and persist updates back. Best for sessions that span many interactions but sit idle between them. The container spins down during idle periods and spins back up when the user returns.</span>
<span data-as="p">Example workloads include a personal project manager with intermittent check-ins, deep research that pauses and resumes over hours, and a customer support agent that loads ticket history across interactions.</span>
<span data-as="p">Tune your provider’s idle timeout to how frequently you expect users to return. Shutting a container down without a <code>SessionStore</code> configured loses the transcript with it, so the store is required for this pattern, not optional.</span>
<span data-as="p">The pattern hinges on resuming a session by ID with a shared store attached:</span>

<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="multi-agent-container" aria-labelledby="_R_279deehkd74t9_"><a href="#multi-agent-container" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Multi-agent container"></a><span id="_R_279deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Multi-agent container</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Run multiple SDK subprocesses inside one container. Best for agents that must collaborate closely, for example multi-agent simulations where the agents interact with each other in a shared environment.</span>
<span data-as="p">Give each agent its own working directory so they do not overwrite each other’s files, and isolate settings loading so per-agent <code>CLAUDE.md</code> files do not leak across agents. See <a href="#multi-tenant-isolation" class="link">Multi-tenant isolation</a> for the specific options.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="provision-the-container" aria-labelledby="_R_2d9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#provision-the-container" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Provision the container"></a><span id="_R_2d9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Provision the container</span></h2>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="container-based-sandboxing" aria-labelledby="_R_2f9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#container-based-sandboxing" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Container-based sandboxing"></a><span id="_R_2f9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Container-based sandboxing</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Run the SDK inside a sandboxed container for process isolation, resource limits, network control, and an ephemeral filesystem.</span>
<span data-as="p">Questions to answer when choosing a provider:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Who runs the sandbox</strong>: a sandbox-as-a-service provider operates the infrastructure for you, while self-hosted options give you software to run on your own.</li>
<li><strong>Cold-start latency</strong>: how long from “create a sandbox” to “ready to accept the first request.” Ephemeral patterns need sub-second starts. Long-running patterns tolerate more.</li>
<li><strong>Persistent storage</strong>: whether the provider offers durable volumes or only ephemeral disk. The hybrid pattern needs durable storage somewhere, whether in the sandbox or alongside it.</li>
<li><strong>Pricing model</strong>: per-second, per-request, or flat hourly billing. Per-second pricing suits bursty ephemeral workloads. Hourly suits long-running sessions.</li>
<li><strong>Networking</strong>: support for custom egress rules, outbound proxies, and private VPC peering for regulated environments.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">For self-hosted options such as Docker, gVisor, and Firecracker, and detailed isolation configuration, see <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/secure-deployment#isolation-technologies" class="link">Isolation Technologies</a>.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="runtime-dependencies" aria-labelledby="_R_2p9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#runtime-dependencies" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Runtime dependencies"></a><span id="_R_2p9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Runtime dependencies</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">The container needs your SDK’s language runtime:</span>
<ul>
<li>Python 3.10+ for the Python SDK, or Node.js 18.14+ for the TypeScript SDK</li>
<li>Both the TypeScript and Python SDKs bundle a native Claude Code binary for most installs, and the spawned CLI needs no separate Node.js install. See the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/quickstart" class="link">quickstart’s install note</a> for the installs that need a separate native Claude Code install.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">The bundled binary is pinned to the SDK package version, so updating the SDK is how you update the CLI. The SDK follows semver: take patch releases continuously and review the <a href="https://github.com/anthropics/claude-agent-sdk-typescript/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">TypeScript</a> or <a href="https://github.com/anthropics/claude-agent-sdk-python/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">Python</a> changelog before taking a minor.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="resources" aria-labelledby="_R_319deehkd74t9_"><a href="#resources" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Resources"></a><span id="_R_319deehkd74t9_" class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Resources</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">1 GiB RAM, 5 GiB disk, and 1 CPU per agent is a reasonable starting point for a freshly started instance. Memory usage grows with session length and tool activity, so size for the session lengths and concurrency you actually need rather than the idle baseline. See <a href="#scaling-and-concurrency" class="link">Scaling and concurrency</a> for how to work out agents per host.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="network" aria-labelledby="_R_359deehkd74t9_"><a href="#network" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Network"></a><span id="_R_359deehkd74t9_" class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Network</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">The SDK needs outbound HTTPS to <code>api.anthropic.com</code>, or to your provider’s regional endpoint when running on Amazon Bedrock or Google Cloud’s Agent Platform. If your agents use <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/mcp" class="link">MCP servers</a> or external tools, they need outbound access to those endpoints as well. For production, route outbound traffic through an egress proxy that enforces domain allowlists, injects credentials, and logs requests. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/secure-deployment" class="link">Secure Deployment</a> for the full pattern.</span>
<span data-as="p">For inbound traffic, expose an HTTP or WebSocket port on the container. Your application handles client requests on that port and calls the SDK internally; the subprocess itself does not listen on the network.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="handle-production-concerns" aria-labelledby="_R_3b9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#handle-production-concerns" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Handle production concerns"></a><span id="_R_3b9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Handle production concerns</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">Work through these decisions before shipping a self-hosted agent.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="session-and-state-persistence" aria-labelledby="_R_3f9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#session-and-state-persistence" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Session and state persistence"></a><span id="_R_3f9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Session and state persistence</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Default local disk is lost on restart, scale-down, or a move to a different node. For any session a user expects to resume, mirror the transcript to durable storage with a <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/session-storage" class="link"><code>SessionStore</code> adapter</a>. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/session-storage#reference-implementations" class="link">Reference implementations</a> for example adapters for an object store, a key-value store, and a database, and a conformance suite for your own.</span>
<span data-as="p">Three things to know about how <code>SessionStore</code> behaves:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Transcripts only</strong>: <code>SessionStore</code> mirrors transcripts, not <code>CLAUDE.md</code> memory files or other working-directory artifacts. Mount a shared volume or sync those separately.</li>
<li><strong>Mirror, not replacement</strong>: the subprocess writes to local disk first, and the SDK forwards a copy of each batch to the store. A fresh session’s local transcript outlives the run; a run resumed from the store deletes its local copy at the end, so the store holds the only durable copy. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/session-storage#dual-write-architecture" class="link">Dual-write architecture</a>.</li>
<li><strong><code>mirror_error</code> messages</strong>: when the SDK can’t deliver a batch to the store, it drops the batch, emits a <code>{ type: &quot;system&quot;, subtype: &quot;mirror_error&quot; }</code> message, and continues the query. Alert on these if store durability matters. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/session-storage#mirror-writes-are-best-effort" class="link">Mirror writes are best-effort</a> for the retry and timeout behavior.</li>
</ul>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="observability" aria-labelledby="_R_3n9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#observability" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Observability"></a><span id="_R_3n9deehkd74t9_" class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Observability</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Agent SDK agents are long-lived processes that spawn tool calls across many API round-trips. Without telemetry you cannot see which tools ran, how long they took, or where a session stalled.</span>
<span data-as="p">The SDK inherits OpenTelemetry configuration from the environment. Set the OTEL environment variables at the container or orchestrator level so every <code>query()</code> call exports spans, metrics, and log events to your collector. The example below enables OTLP export for all three signals. <code>CLAUDE_CODE_ENHANCED_TELEMETRY_BETA</code> is required only for traces; omit it if you export metrics and logs alone.</span>

<span data-as="p">Prompt text and tool inputs are not included in exports by default. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/observability#control-sensitive-data-in-exports" class="link">Control sensitive data in exports</a> for the opt-in flags, and <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/observability" class="link">Observability</a> for the full signal catalog.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="auth-and-secrets" aria-labelledby="_R_419deehkd74t9_"><a href="#auth-and-secrets" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Auth and secrets"></a><span id="_R_419deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Auth and secrets</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Three auth concerns matter at hosting time:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Anthropic API</strong>: the subprocess reads <code>ANTHROPIC_API_KEY</code> from its environment. Supply it from your secret manager, or set <code>ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL</code> to route model calls through a proxy that injects the key outside the container. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/secure-deployment#credential-management" class="link">Credential management</a> for the proxy pattern and <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/quickstart#setup" class="link">Setup in the SDK quickstart</a> for supported authentication methods.</li>
<li><strong>Inbound</strong>: put authentication at a gateway in front of the agent container. The agent should receive pre-authenticated requests and should not be the component that validates user tokens.</li>
<li><strong>Outbound tools</strong>: keep tool credentials out of the agent environment. Route outbound calls through a proxy that injects API keys after the request leaves the container. The agent makes the call; the proxy adds the credential.</li>
</ul>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="scaling-and-concurrency" aria-labelledby="_R_479deehkd74t9_"><a href="#scaling-and-concurrency" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Scaling and concurrency"></a><span id="_R_479deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Scaling and concurrency</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Each session runs in its own subprocess, so concurrency on a host is bounded by how many subprocesses its RAM can hold.</span>
<span data-as="p">Size each host with this formula:</span>
<div class="code-block mt-5 mb-8 not-prose rounded-2xl relative group min-w-0 print:print-color-exact text-gray-950 dark:text-gray-50 codeblock-light border border-gray-950/10 dark:border-white/10 children:my-0! children:shadow-none! children:bg-transparent!"><div class="font-mono whitespace-pre leading-6"><pre class="shiki shiki-themes github-light-default dark-plus" style="background-color:#ffffff;--shiki-dark-bg:#0B0C0E;color:#1f2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4" language="text"><code language="text" numberOfLines="2"><span class="line"><span>agents per host = (host RAM - overhead) / (per-session RAM ceiling)</span></span>
<span class="line"><span></span></span></code></pre></div></div></div></div><div class="code-block-fade-overlay print:hidden" data-fade-overlay="true" aria-hidden="true" style="--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E"></div></div>
<span data-as="p">Measure the per-session ceiling by running a representative session to your target length under your expected tool load and recording peak RSS. The 1 GiB starting point in <a href="#resources" class="link">Resources</a> is a floor, not the ceiling.</span>
<span data-as="p">Horizontal-scale routing depends on your pattern. For long-running sessions, where containers hold many sessions, run a pool of containers behind a load balancer and pin each session to one container using consistent hashing on <code>sessionId</code>. A pinned session keeps hitting the same container, and therefore the same running subprocess, until it is evicted or the container restarts.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="cost" aria-labelledby="_R_4j9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#cost" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Cost"></a><span id="_R_4j9deehkd74t9_" class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Cost</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Anthropic token cost typically dominates container infrastructure cost by an order of magnitude or more. A minimally provisioned container runs roughly $0.05 per hour, while a single long agent session can spend dollars in tokens. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/cost-tracking" class="link">Cost tracking</a> for per-session token accounting.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="multi-tenant-isolation" aria-labelledby="_R_4n9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#multi-tenant-isolation" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Multi-tenant isolation"></a><span id="_R_4n9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Multi-tenant isolation</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Default SDK behavior reads settings and <code>CLAUDE.md</code> memory files from the filesystem. In a shared container that serves multiple tenants, those files can leak one tenant’s context into another tenant’s session.</span>
<span data-as="p">To isolate tenants inside a shared container:</span>
<ul>
<li>Pass <code>settingSources: []</code> in TypeScript or <code>setting_sources=[]</code> in Python to skip user, project, and local settings.</li>
<li>Set <code>CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_AUTO_MEMORY=1</code> in <code>env</code>. <a href="/docs/en/memory#auto-memory" class="link">Auto memory</a> at <code>~/.claude/projects/&lt;project&gt;/memory/</code> loads into the system prompt regardless of <code>settingSources</code>. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/claude-code-features#what-settingsources-does-not-control" class="link">What settingSources does not control</a> for the other inputs that load unconditionally.</li>
<li>Point <code>CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR</code> at a per-tenant directory so tenants do not share the <code>~/.claude.json</code> global config. When each config directory serves one working directory and you don’t pass a <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/session-storage" class="link"><code>SessionStore</code></a>, you can also set <a href="/docs/en/sessions#name-the-project-directory-yourself" class="link"><code>CLAUDE_CODE_PROJECT_DIR_NAME</code></a> in <code>env</code> to keep the transcript paths under it short. Requires TypeScript Agent SDK v0.3.234 or later, or Python Agent SDK v0.2.140 or later.</li>
<li>Use a per-tenant working directory. Pass <code>cwd</code> explicitly on every <code>query()</code> call.</li>
<li>Apply per-tenant egress rules at your proxy, such as distinct outbound IPs, credentials, or domain allowlists, so a compromised tenant cannot exfiltrate data via another tenant’s outbound policy.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">The example below applies the settings, auto memory, config directory, and working directory options together. Construct <code>tenantDir</code> and <code>configDir</code> so each tenant gets a path no other tenant can read. In TypeScript, <code>env</code> replaces the subprocess environment, so spread <code>...process.env</code> to keep inherited variables like <code>PATH</code> and <code>ANTHROPIC_API_KEY</code>. In Python, <code>env</code> is merged on top of the inherited environment.</span>

<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="known-limitations" aria-labelledby="_R_539deehkd74t9_"><a href="#known-limitations" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Known limitations"></a><span id="_R_539deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Known limitations</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">Plan around these in your deployment design.</span>
<!-- --><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px" data-component-part="scroll-area" data-table-wrapper="true" class="min-w-0 [--page-padding:20px] flex w-[calc(100%+(var(--page-padding)*2))] my-[1em] py-[1em] -mx-(--page-padding) max-w-none [contain:inline-size]"><div data-id="base-ui-_R_579deehkd74t9_-viewport" tabindex="-1" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" style="overflow:scroll" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable table"><div role="presentation" style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="flex"><div class="px-(--page-padding) grow max-w-none table"><table class="m-0 min-w-full w-full max-w-none table [&amp;_th]:text-left [&amp;_td[data-numeric]]:tabular-nums [&amp;_td]:min-w-[150px]"><thead><tr><th>Limitation</th><th>What to do</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>No top-level session timeout</td><td>A session doesn’t time out on its own. Set <code>maxTurns</code> in TypeScript or <code>max_turns</code> in Python to bound how many <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/agent-loop#turns-and-messages" class="link">turns</a> the agent takes before stopping.</td></tr><tr><td>Memory growth over long sessions</td><td>Cap session length or recycle subprocesses periodically. See <a href="#scaling-and-concurrency" class="link">Scaling and concurrency</a>.</td></tr><tr><td>Large parallel-subagent fanouts can hit rate limits</td><td>Break work into smaller batches rather than issuing one wide dispatch.</td></tr><tr><td>No per-subagent wall-clock deadline</td><td>Cap each <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/subagents" class="link">subagent</a> with <code>maxTurns</code> in its <code>AgentDefinition</code>. <code>CLAUDE_ASYNC_AGENT_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS</code> sets a stall watchdog that fires when a subagent stops producing output; it isn’t a total-runtime deadline.</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="troubleshoot-deployment-failures" aria-labelledby="_R_599deehkd74t9_"><a href="#troubleshoot-deployment-failures" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Troubleshoot deployment failures"></a><span id="_R_599deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Troubleshoot deployment failures</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">Use this section when an agent that works on your machine fails in a deployed service. Each item below names a failure and links the entry that covers it:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>CLI not found at service start</strong>: in Python, a container or service manager runs your application with a different <code>PATH</code> than your shell, so an install that works locally isn’t visible to the process. In TypeScript, the image build skipped the SDK’s optional dependencies, or <code>pathToClaudeCodeExecutable</code> points at a file that doesn’t exist in the image. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/troubleshooting#clinotfounderror-claude-code-not-found" class="link">Claude Code not found</a>.</li>
<li><strong>CLI present in the image but won’t launch</strong>: Claude Code can’t start from a binary that doesn’t match the container’s architecture or libc, or from a file that lost its execute permission in the image build. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/troubleshooting#cliconnectionerror-failed-to-start-claude-code" class="link">Failed to start Claude Code</a>.</li>
<li><strong>Claude Code process exits mid-run</strong>: the error your application receives depends on the SDK language and on whether the CLI reported an error result first. The entries under <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/troubleshooting#cli-process-exit" class="link">CLI process exit</a> cover each message.</li>
</ul>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="next-steps" aria-labelledby="_R_5f9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#next-steps" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Next steps"></a><span id="_R_5f9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Next steps</span></h2>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://github.com/anthropics/claude-cookbooks/blob/main/claude_agent_sdk/07_Hosting_the_agent.ipynb" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">Hosting cookbook</a>: notebook walkthrough with <a href="https://github.com/anthropics/claude-cookbooks/tree/main/claude_agent_sdk/hosting" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">deployable code</a> for Docker, Modal, and Kubernetes.</li>
<li><a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/session-storage" class="link">Session storage</a>: persist transcripts across hosts with a <code>SessionStore</code> adapter.</li>
<li><a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/observability" class="link">Observability</a>: export OTEL traces, metrics, and logs to your collector.</li>
<li><a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/secure-deployment" class="link">Secure deployment</a>: network controls, credential management, and isolation hardening.</li>
<li><a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/cost-tracking" class="link">Cost tracking</a>: per-session token and cost accounting.</li>
</ul></div><nav id="pagination" data-layout-item="pagination" aria-label="Pagination" class="px-0.5 flex items-center gap-6 text-sm font-semibold text-gray-700 dark:text-gray-200"><a Every running agent is a long-lived process tied to local state, which shapes how you allocate resources, persist sessions, and scale across tenants.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`This page covers self-hosting on your own infrastructure. For deployable Dockerfiles and Kubernetes manifests, see the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/anthropics/claude-cookbooks/tree/main/claude_agent_sdk/hosting`,children:`hosting cookbook`}),`.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`If you don’t need to run the agent loop itself on your own infrastructure, consider `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/managed-agents/overview`,children:`Managed Agents`}),` instead. Anthropic hosts the agent loop, and your application sends events and receives streamed results through the client SDKs or the REST API. Tool execution runs in an Anthropic-managed cloud sandbox or a `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/managed-agents/self-hosted-sandboxes`,children:`self-hosted sandbox`}),` on your own infrastructure.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`2`,id:`the-subprocess-model`,children:`The subprocess model`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Every hosting decision on this page follows from how the SDK runs the agent. When your code calls `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`query()`}),`, the SDK spawns a separate `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`claude`}),` CLI process and talks to it over stdio. That subprocess owns the shell, the working directory, and the JSONL session transcripts on local disk.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(OptimizedImage,{src:`https://mintcdn.com/claude-code/ikqp3_70mqIahteV/images/agent-sdk/hosting-subprocess.svg?fit=max\u0026auto=format\u0026n=ikqp3_70mqIahteV\u0026q=85\u0026s=9dac857ca9d3b1410c3734900c386004`,className:`dark:mint-hidden`,alt:`Request flow: client to your app, which spawns a claude CLI subprocess over stdio inside the container; Best for agents that must collaborate closely, for example multi-agent simulations where the agents interact with each other in a shared environment.`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Give each agent its own working directory so they do not overwrite each other’s files, and isolate settings loading so per-agent `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`CLAUDE.md`}),` files do not leak across agents. See `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#multi-tenant-isolation`,children:`Multi-tenant isolation`}),` for the specific options.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`2`,id:`provision-the-container`,children:`Provision the container`}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`container-based-sandboxing`,children:`Container-based sandboxing`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Run the SDK inside a sandboxed container for process isolation, resource limits, network control, and an ephemeral filesystem.`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Questions to answer when choosing a provider:`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Who runs the sandbox`}),`: a sandbox-as-a-service provider operates the infrastructure for you, while self-hosted options give you software to run on your own.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Cold-start latency`}),`: how long from “create a sandbox” to “ready to accept the first request.” Ephemeral patterns need sub-second starts. Long-running patterns tolerate more.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Persistent storage`}),`: whether the provider offers durable volumes or only ephemeral disk. The hybrid pattern needs durable storage somewhere, whether in the sandbox or alongside it.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Pricing model`}),`: per-second, per-request, or flat hourly billing. Per-second pricing suits bursty ephemeral workloads. Hourly suits long-running sessions.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Networking`}),`: support for custom egress rules, outbound proxies, and private VPC peering for regulated environments.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`For self-hosted options such as Docker, gVisor, and Firecracker, and detailed isolation configuration, see `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/secure-deployment#isolation-technologies`,children:`Isolation Technologies`}),`.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`runtime-dependencies`,children:`Runtime dependencies`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`The container needs your SDK’s language runtime:`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsx(_components.li,{children:`Python 3.10+ for the Python SDK, or Node.js 18.14+ for the TypeScript SDK`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`Both the TypeScript and Python SDKs bundle a native Claude Code binary for most installs, and the spawned CLI needs no separate Node.js install. See the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/quickstart`,children:`quickstart’s install note`}),` for the installs that need a separate native Claude Code install.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The bundled binary is pinned to the SDK package version, so updating the SDK is how you update the CLI. The SDK follows semver: take patch releases continuously and review the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/anthropics/claude-agent-sdk-typescript/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md`,children:`TypeScript`}),` or `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/anthropics/claude-agent-sdk-python/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md`,children:`Python`}),` changelog before taking a minor.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`resources`,children:`Resources`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`1 GiB RAM, 5 GiB disk, and 1 CPU per agent is a reasonable starting point for a freshly started instance. Memory usage grows with session length and tool activity, so size for the session lengths and concurrency you actually need rather than the idle baseline. See `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#scaling-and-concurrency`,children:`Scaling and concurrency`}),` for how to work out agents per host.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`network`,children:`Network`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The SDK needs outbound HTTPS to `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`api.anthropic.com`}),`, or to your provider’s regional endpoint when running on Amazon Bedrock or Google Cloud’s Agent Platform. If your agents use `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/mcp`,children:`MCP servers`}),` or external tools, they need outbound access to those endpoints as well. For production, route outbound traffic through an egress proxy that enforces domain allowlists, injects credentials, and logs requests. See `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/secure-deployment`,children:`Secure Deployment`}),` for the full pattern.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`For inbound traffic, expose an HTTP or WebSocket port on the container. Your application handles client requests on that port and calls the SDK internally; the subprocess itself does not listen on the network.`}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`2`,id:`handle-production-concerns`,children:`Handle production concerns`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Work through these decisions before shipping a self-hosted agent.`}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`session-and-state-persistence`,children:`Session and state persistence`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Default local disk is lost on restart, scale-down, or a move to a different node. For any session a user expects to resume, mirror the transcript to durable storage with a `,_jsxs(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/session-storage`,children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SessionStore`}),` adapter`]}),`. See `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/session-storage#reference-implementations`,children:`Reference implementations`}),` for example adapters for an object store, a key-value store, and a database, and a conformance suite for your own.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Three things to know about how `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SessionStore`}),` behaves:`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Transcripts only`}),`: `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SessionStore`}),` mirrors transcripts, not `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`CLAUDE.md`}),` memory files or other working-directory artifacts. Mount a shared volume or sync those separately.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Mirror, not replacement`}),`: the subprocess writes to local disk first, and the SDK forwards a copy of each batch to the store. A fresh session’s local transcript outlives the run; a run resumed from the store deletes its local copy at the end, so the store holds the only durable copy. See `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/session-storage#dual-write-architecture`,children:`Dual-write architecture`}),`.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsxs(_components.strong,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`mirror_error`}),` messages`]}),`: when the SDK can’t deliver a batch to the store, it drops the batch, emits a `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`{ type: \"system\", subtype: \"mirror_error\" }`}),` message, and continues the query. Alert on these if store durability matters. See `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/session-storage#mirror-writes-are-best-effort`,children:`Mirror writes are best-effort`}),` for the retry and timeout behavior.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`observability`,children:`Observability`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Agent SDK agents are long-lived processes that spawn tool calls across many API round-trips. Without telemetry you cannot see which tools ran, how long they took, or where a session stalled.`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The SDK inherits OpenTelemetry configuration from the environment. Set the OTEL environment variables at the container or orchestrator level so every `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`query()`}),` call exports spans, metrics, and log events to your collector. The example below enables OTLP export for all three signals. `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`CLAUDE_CODE_ENHANCED_TELEMETRY_BETA`}),` is required only for traces; omit it if you export metrics and logs alone.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(CodeBlock,{filename:`.env`,numberOfLines:`7`,language:`shellscript`,children:_jsx(_components.pre,{className:`shiki shiki-themes github-light-default dark-plus`,style:{backgroundColor:`#ffffff`,\"--shiki-dark-bg\":`#0B0C0E`,color:`#1f2328`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#D4D4D4`},language:`shellscript`,children:_jsxs(_components.code,{language:`shellscript`,numberOfLines:`7`,children:[_jsxs(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:[_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#1F2328`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#9CDCFE`},children:`CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_TELEMETRY`}),_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#CF222E`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#D4D4D4`},children:`=`}),_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#0A3069`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#CE9178`},children:`1`})]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:[_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#1F2328`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#9CDCFE`},children:`CLAUDE_CODE_ENHANCED_TELEMETRY_BETA`}),_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#CF222E`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#D4D4D4`},children:`=`}),_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#0A3069`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#CE9178`},children:`1`})]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:[_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#1F2328`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#9CDCFE`},children:`OTEL_TRACES_EXPORTER`}),_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#CF222E`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#D4D4D4`},children:`=`}),_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#0A3069`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#CE9178`},children:`otlp`})]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:[_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#1F2328`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#9CDCFE`},children:`OTEL_METRICS_EXPORTER`}),_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#CF222E`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#D4D4D4`},children:`=`}),_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#0A3069`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#CE9178`},children:`otlp`})]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:[_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#1F2328`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#9CDCFE`},children:`OTEL_LOGS_EXPORTER`}),_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#CF222E`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#D4D4D4`},children:`=`}),_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#0A3069`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#CE9178`},children:`otlp`})]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:[_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#1F2328`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#9CDCFE`},children:`OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_PROTOCOL`}),_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#CF222E`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#D4D4D4`},children:`=`}),_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#0A3069`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#CE9178`},children:`http/protobuf`})]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:[_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#1F2328`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#9CDCFE`},children:`OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT`}),_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#CF222E`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#D4D4D4`},children:`=`}),_jsx(_components.span,{style:{color:`#0A3069`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#CE9178`},children:`http://collector.example.com:4318`})]}),`\n`]})})}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Prompt text and tool inputs are not included in exports by default. See `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/observability#control-sensitive-data-in-exports`,children:`Control sensitive data in exports`}),` for the opt-in flags, and `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/observability`,children:`Observability`}),` for the full signal catalog.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`auth-and-secrets`,children:`Auth and secrets`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Three auth concerns matter at hosting time:`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Anthropic API`}),`: the subprocess reads `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`}),` from its environment. Supply it from your secret manager, or set `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`}),` to route model calls through a proxy that injects the key outside the container. See `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/secure-deployment#credential-management`,children:`Credential management`}),` for the proxy pattern and `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/quickstart#setup`,children:`Setup in the SDK quickstart`}),` for supported authentication methods.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Inbound`}),`: put authentication at a gateway in front of the agent container. The agent should receive pre-authenticated requests and should not be the component that validates user tokens.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Outbound tools`}),`: keep tool credentials out of the agent environment. Route outbound calls through a proxy that injects API keys after the request leaves the container. The agent makes the call; the proxy adds the credential.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`scaling-and-concurrency`,children:`Scaling and concurrency`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Each session runs in its own subprocess, so concurrency on a host is bounded by how many subprocesses its RAM can hold.`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Size each host with this formula:`}),`\n`,_jsx(CodeBlock,{filename:``,numberOfLines:`2`,language:`text`,children:_jsx(_components.pre,{className:`shiki shiki-themes github-light-default dark-plus`,style:{backgroundColor:`#ffffff`,\"--shiki-dark-bg\":`#0B0C0E`,color:`#1f2328`,\"--shiki-dark\":`#D4D4D4`},language:`text`,children:_jsxs(_components.code,{language:`text`,numberOfLines:`2`,children:[_jsx(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:_jsx(_components.span,{children:`agents per host = (host RAM - overhead) / (per-session RAM ceiling)`})}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:_jsx(_components.span,{})})]})})}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Measure the per-session ceiling by running a representative session to your target length under your expected tool load and recording peak RSS. The 1 GiB starting point in `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#resources`,children:`Resources`}),` is a floor, not the ceiling.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Horizontal-scale routing depends on your pattern. For long-running sessions, where containers hold many sessions, run a pool of containers behind a load balancer and pin each session to one container using consistent hashing on `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`sessionId`}),`. A pinned session keeps hitting the same container, and therefore the same running subprocess, until it is evicted or the container restarts.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`cost`,children:`Cost`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Anthropic token cost typically dominates container infrastructure cost by an order of magnitude or more. A minimally provisioned container runs roughly $0.05 per hour, while a single long agent session can spend dollars in tokens. See `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/cost-tracking`,children:`Cost tracking`}),` for per-session token accounting.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`multi-tenant-isolation`,children:`Multi-tenant isolation`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Default SDK behavior reads settings and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`CLAUDE.md`}),` memory files from the filesystem. In a shared container that serves multiple tenants, those files can leak one tenant’s context into another tenant’s session.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`To isolate tenants inside a shared container:`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`Pass `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`settingSources: []`}),` in TypeScript or `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`setting_sources=[]`}),` in Python to skip user, project, and local settings.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`Set `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_AUTO_MEMORY=1`}),` in `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`env`}),`. `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/memory#auto-memory`,children:`Auto memory`}),` at `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`~/.claude/projects/\u003cproject>/memory/`}),` loads into the system prompt regardless of `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`settingSources`}),`. See `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/claude-code-features#what-settingsources-does-not-control`,children:`What settingSources does not control`}),` for the other inputs that load unconditionally.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`Point `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`}),` at a per-tenant directory so tenants do not share the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`~/.claude.json`}),` global config. When each config directory serves one working directory and you don’t pass a `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/session-storage`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SessionStore`})}),`, you can also set `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/sessions#name-the-project-directory-yourself`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`CLAUDE_CODE_PROJECT_DIR_NAME`})}),` in `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`env`}),` to keep the transcript paths under it short. Requires TypeScript Agent SDK v0.3.234 or later, or Python Agent SDK v0.2.140 or later.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`Use a per-tenant working directory. Pass `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`cwd`}),` explicitly on every `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`query()`}),` call.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.li,{children:`Apply per-tenant egress rules at your proxy, such as distinct outbound IPs, credentials, or domain allowlists, so a compromised tenant cannot exfiltrate data via another tenant’s outbound policy.`}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The example below applies the settings, auto memory, config directory, and working directory options together. Construct `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`tenantDir`}),` and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`configDir`}),` so each tenant gets a path no other tenant can read. In TypeScript, `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`env`}),` replaces the subprocess environment, so spread `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`...process.env`}),` to keep inherited variables like `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`PATH`}),` and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`}),`. In Python, `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`env`}),` is merged on top of the inherited environment.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(CodeGroup,{children:[_jsx(CodeBlock,{filename:`TypeScript`,numberOfLines:`20`,language:`typescript`,children:_jsx(_components.pre,{className:`shiki shiki-themes
